that is contained in

another 7-simplex,
t
, that contains

and no other vertices from
\mathcal{S}
. Then

is one of the other simplices from
Equation

that has been replaced by two simplices

. We will have that

.

that contains

,

. Then

are not present in

Next we turn to consider simplices which share exactly three vertices with
\mathcal{S}
.
Again, here we must consider two types of simplices.
First we consider simplices which share the 2-simplices
[xx^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}]
or

with
\mathcal{S}

There are 32 such 7-simplices (16 for each 2-simplex).
These 7-simplices are not cut open by the process of adding the dummy vertices, because the 2-simplices they share with
\mathcal{S}
are left intact by the process of cutting
\mathcal{S}
open.

The other type of simplex we must consider are those that share the 2-simplices
[xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
or
[xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}]

\mathcal{S}

Again, there are 32 such simplices.
These 7-simplices are removed from the complex by the process of adding the dummy vertices, because the 2-simplices they share with
\mathcal{S}
are no longer present in the complex after
\mathcal{S}
has been cut open.
We will consider first the 7-simplices which share the 2-simplex

with

The edges between the dummy vertices
\{x_{3}\dots x_{10}\}
and the rest of the complex are designed to ensure there are no gaps between the simplices that replace these simplices in

.
We will show how this works for the simplex

we can see that the vertex

.

•

[xb_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this becomes
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x_{1}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this becomes
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x_{1}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xb_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this becomes
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this becomes
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xb_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this becomes
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this becomes
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xb_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this becomes
[xb_{3}x^{\prime}_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[xb_{3}x_{9}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}x_{8}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this becomes
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}]
which is shared with
[x_{9}b_{3}x^{\prime}a_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}x^{\prime\prime\prime}x_{8}]
.

So there are no resulting holes in the complex.

So we have shown that each of the 7-simplices in the cycle
c_{0}
are still in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, or have been removed and replaced with a pair of simplices which ‘fill in’ the same point in the complex, leaving no gaps.
Next we consider the new simplices added by the dummy vertices, which are not simply replacing simplices from
c_{0}
.
That is, simplices which contain two or more dummy vertices.
The thickening procedure is designed precisely so that every new simplex that is added shares every facet (except those that bound the hole
\mathfrak{g}_{7})
with another simplex in the complex, leaving no gaps (see
Section

7.8
).
So, as claimed, the only hole we have cut in the copy of
\mathfrak{g}_{8}
is that given by the copy of
\mathfrak{g}_{7}
formed out of the vertices from
\mathcal{S}
along with the dummy vertices (the complex
\mathcal{C}_{0}
has trivial homology despite being formed by cutting a hole in
\mathfrak{g}_{8}
becaues the process of cutting a hole takes a sphere, which has non-trivial homology, to a hyper-plane, which has trivial homology).

Precisely the same argument applies to cutting open the cycle for the basis state
\ket{1000}
.
So we are left with complexes which are each triangulations of a 7-dimensional hyper-planes with boundaries of the same size.
Gluing these hyper-planes together along the boundaries clearly gives a triangulation of
S^{7}
as claimed.
Moreover, at every step when constructing this complex we have added edges, and every simplex induced by the edge.
So the resulting complex is 2-determined, and therefore a clique-complex.

∎

Lemma D.2
.

The procedure outlined in
Section

8.3.10
for cutting and gluing more than three three-qubit cycles results in a clique complex
\Kcyc
which triangulates
S^{5}
.

Proof.

For concreteness we will consider the state
-5\ket{011}+4\ket{100}+3\ket{101}
throughout the proof, but it trivially generalises to other three-qubit states.

First we demonstrate that applying the cutting process from
Section

8.3.8
to the cycle
c_{0}
corresponding to the basis state
\ket{011}
results in a complex
\mathcal{C}_{0}
which is a triangulation of
S^{5}
with a copy of
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
cut into it by the dummy vertices.
Is is immediate from
Section

8.3.8
that the dummy vertices
\{x_{1}\dots x_{9}\}
along with the original vertices
x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}
form a copy of
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
.
So all that remains is to show that no additional holes were formed in the process of constructing this hole.

As in the proof of the previous lemma, we will do this by showing that the facet of every simplex in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
is either shared with another simplex in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, or is a face of the hole
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
.
Again, we will only need to show this for maximal simplices, which are 5-simplices in this case.

In this case the hole in
c_{0}
is cut into the 2-simplex
\mathcal{S}=[xx^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
.
So any simplex from
c_{0}
that shares no lower simplices with
\mathcal{S}
is present in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, and shares all facets with other simplices in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

We also have immediately that any simplex that shares exactly one vertex with
\mathcal{S}
is still in the complex
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and shares all facets with other simplices in the complex because the lower simplices they contain are unaffected by the cutting procedure.
Moreover, any simplex that shares only the 1-simplices
[xx^{\prime\prime}]
or
[x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
with
\mathcal{S}
is still in the complex
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and shares all facets with other simplices in the complex for the same reason.

Next consider 5-simplices which share the 1-simplex
[xx^{\prime}]
with
\mathcal{S}
.
There are eight such 5-simplices:

\begin{split}&[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\\
&[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\end{split}

(39)

The dummy vertex
x_{1}
is connected to the original vertices
\{x,a_{3},a_{4},x^{\prime},b^{\prime}_{3},b^{\prime}_{4},b^{\prime\prime}_{2},b^{\prime\prime}_{3},b^{\prime\prime}_{4}\}
(see
Section

8.3.8
).
Therefore each simplex
s\in c_{0}
from
Equation

39
is replaced in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
by two simplices – one
s^{\prime}
containing the 1-simplex
[xx_{1}]
and the other
s^{\prime\prime}
containing the 1-simplex
[x^{\prime}x_{1}]
.
The simplices
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
share the 4-simplex given by removing the vertices
x
and
x^{\prime}
from the original vertex set, and adding the vertex
x_{1}
.
In
c_{0}
the other facets of each simplex
s
in
Equation

39
were shared with either:

•

another 5-simplex,
t
, that contains one of
x,x^{\prime}
. Since
t
only contains one of
x,x^{\prime}
we have that
t\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, and moreover
t
will now share a facet with either
s
or
s^{\prime\prime}
(depending on whether it is
x
or
x^{\prime}
that is contained in
t
)

•

another 5-simplex that contains
x,x^{\prime}
but not
x^{\prime\prime}
. Then
t
is one of the other simplices from
Equation

39
that has now been replaced by two simplices
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
. We will have that
t^{\prime}
shares a facet with
s^{\prime}
, and
t^{\prime\prime}
shares a facet with
s^{\prime\prime}

•

another 5-simplex that contains
x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}
– we will consider such 5-simplices next

So while the 5-simplices in
Equation

39
are not present in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, they are replaced by new 5-simplices that share every facet with a simplex that is in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

Next we consider simplices that contain
\mathcal{S}
.
There are eight such 5-simplices:

\begin{split}&[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\\
&[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]\textrm{\ \ \ \ \ \ }[xa_{4}x^{\prime}b_{4}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{4}^{\prime\prime}]\end{split}

(40)

The edges between the dummy vertices
x_{2}\dots x_{9}
are designed to ensure there are no gaps between the simplices that replace these simplices in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.
We will show how this works for the simplex
s=[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, the other simplices can be analysed in a similar manner.
From
Section

8.3.8
we can see that the simplex
x_{2}
is connected to every vertex in this simplex.
Therefore this 5-simplex is cut open and forms two new 5-simplices:
s=[xa_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
,
s=[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
.
There are six facets of
s
that we need to consider:

•

[a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet becomes
[a_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
s^{\prime}
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this is unchanged, and is shared with
[a_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xx^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet becomes
[xx_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
[xx_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this facet becomes
[x_{2}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
[x_{2}x_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xa_{3}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet is unchanged, and is shared with
[xa_{3}b_{2}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this facet becomes
[x_{2}a_{3}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
and is shared with
s^{\prime}
.

•

[xa_{3}x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet becomes
[xa_{3}x_{2}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
[xa_{3}x_{2}x_{8}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this facet becomes
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, and is shared with
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}x_{8}x^{\prime\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet becomes
[xa_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
[xa_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x_{1}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this facet becomes
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
, and is shared with
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x_{1}b_{3}^{\prime\prime}]
.

•

[xa_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
– in
s^{\prime}
this facet becomes
[xa_{3}x_{2}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
, which is shared with
[xa_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}x_{4}]
. In
s^{\prime\prime}
this facet becomes
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}]
, and is shared with
[x_{2}a_{3}x^{\prime}b_{3}^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}x_{4}]
.

So there are no resulting holes in the complex.
We have shown that each of the 5-simplices in the cycle
c_{0}
is either still in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, or have been removed and replaced with a pair of simplices which share every facet with another facet in the complex, leaving no gaps.
Now we consider the new simplices added by the dummy vertices, which are not simply replacing simplices from
c_{0}
.
That is, simplices which contain two or more dummy vertices.
It can be seen by inspection of
Figure

25
,
Figure

26
and
Figure

27
that every cycle involving the dummy vertices can be continuously deformed to a cycle which includes only the original vertices.
Therefore, every facet of every new simplex must be shared with another simplex in the complex, except the simplices that bound the hole
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
(the complex
\mathcal{C}_{0}
has trivial homology despite beging formed by cutting a hole in
\mathfrak{g}_{6}
because the process of cutting a hole takes a sphere to a hyper-plane).

Precisely the same argument applies to cutting open five more copies of
c_{0}
, and to cutting open four copies of
c_{1}
(the cycle corresponding to
\ket{100}
) and three copies of
c_{2}
(the cycle corresponding to
\ket{101}
).
Therefore if we label the dummy vertices as follows:

•

c_{0}^{(1)}
:
x_{1}\dots x_{9}

•

c_{0}^{(2)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{6}\dots x_{13}

•

c_{0}^{(3)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{10}\dots x_{17}

•

c_{0}^{(4)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{14}\dots x_{21}

•

c_{0}^{(5)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{18}\dots x_{25}

•

c_{1}^{(1)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{22}\dots x_{29}

•

c_{1}^{(2)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{26}\dots x_{33}

•

c_{1}^{(3)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{30}\dots x_{37}

•

c_{1}^{(4)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{34}\dots x_{41}

•

c_{2}^{(1)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{38}\dots x_{45}

•

c_{2}^{(2)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{42}\dots x_{49}

•

c_{2}^{(3)}
:
x_{1}
&
x_{46}\dots x_{49}
&
x_{2}\dots x_{5}

Then we are taking 12 copies of the hyper plane, and gluing them along common boundaries.
However, care has to be taken because this way of gluing together hyperplanes does not glue together the entire boundary (as in the case when we are just gluing two hyperplanes together), instead it only glues along parts of the boundaries – see
Figure

31
.
This procedure therefore leads to a triangulation of
S^{5}
with a number of holes in it.
To fill in the holes we add additional dummy vertices, connected up to the existing dummy vertices – see
Figure

32
and
Figure

33
.
It can be checked that this process of identifying vertices and adding additional dummy vertices closes every hole in the surface of the triangulation of
S^{5}
, and therefore gives a complex which is a triangulation of
S^{5}
as claimed.
Moreover, at every point when constructing the complex whenever we have added an edge we have also added every simplex containing that edge, so the resulting complex is 2-determined, and hence a clique complex.

∎
